Use httr2 to download the CMS datasets and read them into R for analysis. The datasets include information on Medicare spending, provider payments, and beneficiary demographics.

The possible scores are pretty different across measures. It might be best to break up the data into separate dataframes for each measure to make it easier to analyze and visualize.

time_in_ed_df <- clean_df |> 
    filter(measure_name == "Average (median) time all patients spent in the emergency department before leaving from the visit, including psychiatric/mental health patients and patients who were transferred to another facility. A lower number of minutes is better")

check missingness or “Not Available” values for this measure

clean_time_in_ed_df <- time_in_ed_df |> 
    mutate(
        score = if_else(score == "Not Available", NA_character_, score),
        score = as.numeric(score),
        state = fct(state)
    )

library(naniar)

check distiribution of scores for this measure

clean_time_in_ed_df |> 
    ggplot(aes(x = score)) +
    geom_histogram() +
    facet_wrap(vars(state))
`stat_bin()` using `bins = 30`. Pick better value `binwidth`.
Warning: Removed 616 rows containing non-finite outside the scale range
(`stat_bin()`).

plot median by state. also want to know sample size for each state to see if there are any states with very small sample sizes that might be skewing the results.

clean_time_in_ed_df |> 
    group_by(state) |> 
    summarize(
        median_time = median(score, na.rm = TRUE),
        n = n()
    ) |> 
    ggplot(aes(x = fct_reorder(state, median_time), y = median_time)) +
    geom_col() +
    geom_text(aes(label = n), vjust = -0.5) +
    theme(axis.text.x = element_text(angle = 90, hjust = 1))
